\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\beta}}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{2\sigma}\left|u_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}}{\sigma}\right)\frac{d_{\Gamma}}{\sigma}\right|\|\mathbf{X}\|\sqrt{\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1})}.

From (R2) and (R4) it follows that
u_{0}(s)s=\rho_{0}^{\prime}(s)
is bounded
and for
(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N
of
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
,
we have that
1/\sigma
and
\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta})^{-1})
are uniformly bounded.
This means there exists a universal constant
0<C_{1}<\infty
, such that

\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\beta}}\right\|\leq C_{1}\|\mathbf{X}\|.

Since
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|<\infty
, by dominated convergence, it follows that
for
(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N
of
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
, it holds that

\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\beta}}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\beta}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s}),

and that
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\bm{\beta}
is continous at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
.
Furthermore, from (
11.21
) we find

\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\theta_{j}}=\frac{1}{2\sigma}\rho^{\prime}_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}}{\sigma}\right)(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}}{\partial\theta_{j}}\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}).

for any
j=1,\ldots,l
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma 11.2 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
,
we find

\left|(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}}{\partial\theta_{j}}\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\right|\leq d_{\Gamma}^{2}\left\|\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right\|\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}).

Furthermore, according to (V4),
the mapping
\bm{\theta}\mapsto\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta})/|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta})|^{1/k}
is continuously differentiable.
This means that
there exists a universal constant
0<M_{1}<\infty
, such that

\max_{1\leq j\leq l}\sup_{(\bm{\beta},\bm{\theta})\in N}\left\|\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right\|\leq M_{1}.

(11.25)

We find that

\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right\|\leq\frac{\sigma M_{1}}{2}\left|\rho^{\prime}_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}}{\sigma}\right)\right|\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}^{-1}).

From (R2) and (R4), it follows that
\rho_{0}^{\prime}(s)s^{2}
is bounded
and for
(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N
of
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
,
we have that
\sigma
and
\lambda_{1}(\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta})^{-1})
are uniformly bounded.
This means there exists a universal constant
0<C_{2}<\infty
, such that

\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right\|\leq C_{2},

for all
j=1,\ldots,l
.
By dominated convergence, it follows that
for
(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N
of
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
, it holds that

\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\theta}}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s}),

and that
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\bm{\theta}
is continous at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
.
Finally, from (
11.21
) we obtain

\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}=-\frac{1}{\sigma}\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}}{\sigma}\right)\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}}{\sigma}\right).

From (R2) and (R4), it follows that
\rho_{0}^{\prime}(s)s
is bounded,
and for
(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N
of
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
,
we have that
1/\sigma
is uniformly bounded.
This means there exists a universal constant
0<C_{3}<\infty
, such that

\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}\right\|\leq C_{3}.

By dominated convergence, it follows that
for
(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N
of
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
, it holds that

\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s}),

and that
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\sigma
is continous at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
.
∎

For convenience we state the following result about spherically contoured densities,
e.g., see Lemma 5.1 in
[
20
]
.
This lemma uses the commutation matrix
\mathbf{K}_{k,k}
, which
is the
k^{2}\times k^{2}
block matrix with the
(i,j)
-block being equal to the
k\times k
matrix
\mathbf{\Delta}_{ji}

consisting of zero’s except a 1 at entry
(j,i)
.
A useful property (e.g., see
[
24
, Section 3.7]
) is that for any
k\times k
matrix
\mathbf{A}
,
it holds that

\mathbf{K}_{k,k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A})=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A}^{T}).

(11.26)

Lemma 5
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{z}
has a
k
-variate elliptical contoured density
defined in (
8.4
), with parameters
\bm{\mu}=\mathbf{0}
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}=\mathbf{I}_{k}
.
Then
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
is independent of
\|\mathbf{z}\|
,
has mean zero and covariance matrix
(1/k)\mathbf{I}_{k}
.
Furthermore,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{u}]=\mathbf{0}

and

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T})^{T}\right]=\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k})+\sigma_{2}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T},

where
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=(k(k+2))^{-1}
.

Proof.

See e.g. the proof of Lemma 5.1 in Lopuhaä
[
20
]
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Suppose that
P
satisfies (E) for some
\bm{\zeta}^{*}=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\bm{\Theta}

and suppose that
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|<\infty
.
Suppose that
\rho_{0}
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfy (R2), (R4) and (V4), respectively.
Let
\bm{\zeta}_{0}=(\bm{\beta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{0})
be the pair of initial functionals
satisfying
(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})
.
Let
\sigma(P)
be the unique solution of (
3.8
)
and let
\Lambda_{0}
be defined in (
11.22
) with
\Psi_{0}
from (
11.21
).
Then,

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))}{\partial\bm{\zeta}}=\mathbf{0},

and

D_{0,\sigma}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))}{\partial\sigma}=-\frac{1}{\sigma(P)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right],

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma(P)
.

Proof.

Write
\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}=(\bm{\beta}_{0,P},\bm{\theta}_{0,P})=(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))
and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
Because
\rho_{0}
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfy (R2), (R4) and (V4), respectively, and
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|<\infty
,
according to Lemma
4
,
we have that
\Lambda_{0}
is continuously differentiable at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})

and that we may interchange integration and differentiation in
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\bm{\beta}

at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})
.
With
u_{0}(s)=\rho_{0}^{\prime}(s)/s
, we find that

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\beta}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\beta}}=-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{P}}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma,0}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{0,P})\right],

where
d_{\Gamma,0}=d(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{0,P},\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P}))
,
as defined in (
7.1
),
with
\bm{\Gamma}
defined in (
3.1
).
Since
\bm{\beta}_{0,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
is a point of symmetry of
P
, it follows that

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\beta}}=\mathbf{0}.

(11.27)

According to Lemma
4
,
we may also interchange integration and differentiation in
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\bm{\theta}

at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})
.
For any
j=1,\ldots,l
,
we find that

\begin{split}D_{0,j}&=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{P}}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma,0}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{e}_{0,P}^{T}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P})^{-1}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P})^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{0,P}\right],\end{split}

(11.28)

where
\mathbf{e}_{0,P}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{0,P}
.
Since
(\bm{\beta}_{0,P},\bm{\theta}_{0,P})=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})
and

\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P})=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})/|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})|^{1/k}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
it follows that
d_{\Gamma,0}=d_{\Gamma}^{*}
, where

(d_{\Gamma}^{*})^{2}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}).

(11.29)

Hence, the expectation on the right hand side of (
11.28
) can be written as

|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[u_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)(\mathbf{e}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{e}^{*}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right]

where
\mathbf{e}^{*}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
.
The inner expectation is the conditional expectation
of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
, which has the same distribution as
\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
,
where
\mathbf{z}
has spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
.
This means that the inner expection can be written as

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}\right],

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
.
Next, let
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
and apply Lemma
5
.
It follows that this expectation is equal to

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\text{tr}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\frac{1}{k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right).\end{split}

Because for each
j=1,\ldots,l
,

\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}}{\partial\theta_{j}}=\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}/|\mathbf{V}|^{1/k}}{\partial\theta_{j}}=-\frac{1}{k}|\mathbf{V}|^{-1/k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}}{\partial\bm{\theta}_{j}}\right)\mathbf{V}+|\mathbf{V}|^{-1/k}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}}{\partial\bm{\theta}_{j}},

together with
\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})=\mathbf{\Sigma}
, it follows that

\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\frac{\partial\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)=0,

for all
j=1,2,\ldots,l
.
This means that

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\theta}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\theta}}=\mathbf{0}.

(11.30)

Together with (
11.27
) this proves part one.

According to Lemma
4
,
we may also interchange integration and differentiation in
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\sigma
.
We find that

D_{0,\sigma}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{P}}\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma,0}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{\Gamma,0}}{\sigma_{P}}\right],

where
d_{\Gamma,0}=d(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{0,P},\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P}))
,
as defined in (
7.1
),
with
\bm{\Gamma}
defined in (
3.1
).
As before, it follows that
d_{\Gamma,0}=d_{\Gamma}^{*}
, where
d_{\Gamma}^{*}
is defined in (
11.29
),
so that

D_{0,\sigma}=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{P}}\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right]=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{P}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right].

Then, the inner expectation on the right hand side is the conditional expectation
of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
, which has the same distribution as
\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
,
where
\mathbf{z}
has spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
.
This means that

D_{0,\sigma}=-\frac{1}{\sigma_{P}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]<0,

(11.31)

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Suppose that
P
satisfies (E) for some
(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\bm{\Theta}

and suppose that
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|<\infty
.
Suppose that
\rho_{0}
satisfies (R2) and (R4), and that
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4).
Let
\bm{\zeta}_{0}=(\bm{\beta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{0})
be the pair of initial functionals
satisfying
(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})
,
and suppose that
\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0},P)
exists.
Let
\sigma(P_{h,\mathbf{s}})
be the solution of equation (
3.8
) with
P=P_{h,\mathbf{s}}
,
and suppose that for all
\mathbf{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{kq}
,
\sigma(P_{h,\mathbf{s}})\to\sigma(P)
, as
h\downarrow 0
,
where
\sigma(P)
is a solution of (
3.8
).
Suppose that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]>0
,
where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma(P)
.
Then, for
\mathbf{s}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{kq}
,

\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\sigma,P)=\frac{\sigma(P)}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]}\Big\{\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)-b_{0}\Big\},

where
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*})
.

Proof.

From Lemmas
4
and
6
,
we have that
\Lambda_{0}
is continuously differentiable
at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
with

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}=\mathbf{0}
and

D_{0,\sigma}=-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]/\sigma(P)<0
.
Since
(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})
and

\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})/|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})|^{1/k}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
it follows that

\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))=\rho_{0}\left(\frac{d(\mathbf{y}_{0},\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*},\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k})}{\sigma(P)}\right)-b_{0}=\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)-b_{0},

(11.32)

where
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*})
.
The lemma now follows immediately from Lemma
3
.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Suppose that
\rho_{1}
satisfies (R2) and (R4).
Let
\sigma(P)
be a solution of (
3.8
) and let
\Psi=(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}},\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}})
, as defined in (
7.3
).
Then there exist
0<C_{1}<\infty
, only depending on
P
and
\sigma(P)
, such that

\|\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))\|\leq C_{1}\|\mathbf{X}\|
.
If in addition,
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4), then there exist
0<C_{2}<\infty
,
only depending on
P
and
\sigma(P)
, such that
\|\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))\|\leq C_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof is completely similar to that of Lemma 11.2 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
.
∎

Lemma 9
.

Let
\Lambda
be defined by (
8.2
) with
\Psi
defined in (
7.3
)
and let
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
.
Suppose that
\rho_{1}
satisfies (R2) and (R5) and
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V5).
Let
\sigma(P)
be a solution of (
3.8
) and let
\bm{\xi}(P)\in\mathfrak{D}
be a local minimum of
R_{P}(\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}))
.
Let
N\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\bm{\Theta}\times(0,\infty)
be an open neighborhood of
(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))
.
Then
\Lambda
is continuous differentiable at
(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P)
and
for all
(\bm{\xi},\sigma)\in N
,

\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\xi}}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\xi}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s}).

Proof.

Let
(\bm{\xi},\sigma)\in N
.
Consider
\bm{\xi}\mapsto\Lambda(\bm{\xi},\sigma)
with
\sigma\in(0,\infty)
fixed.
The proof of

\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\xi}}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\bm{\xi}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s}),

and that
\partial\Lambda/\partial\bm{\xi}
is continuous at
(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))
,
is completely similar to that of Lemma 11.3 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
,
taking into account that
\sigma
is uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity.

Next consider
\sigma\mapsto\Lambda(\bm{\xi},\sigma)
with
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\bm{\Theta}
fixed.
From (
7.3
), we find that

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}&=-u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)\frac{d}{\sigma^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\\
\frac{\partial\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}&=-u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)\frac{d}{\sigma^{2}}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{1,j}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}),\end{split}

for
j=1,\ldots,l
, where
d^{2}=(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})

and
\mathbf{H}_{j}
is defined in (
7.4
).
Taking into account that
\sigma
is uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity,
similar to the proof of Lemma 11.3 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
,
we obtain

\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}\right\|\leq C_{1}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\frac{\partial\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}\right\|\leq C_{2},

for constants
0<C_{1},C_{2}<\infty
only depending on
P
.
Hence, it follows by dominated convergence that for
(\bm{\xi},\sigma)
in the neighborhood
N

of
(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))
, it holds that

\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)}{\partial\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s}),

and that
\partial\Lambda/\partial\sigma
is continuous at
(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))
.
∎

Lemma 10
.

Suppose that
P
satisfies (E) for some
(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\bm{\Theta}

and that
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
.
Suppose that
\rho_{1}
satisfies (R2) and (R5) and that
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V5) and has
a linear structure (
7.5
).
Let
\sigma(P)
be the solution of (
3.8
) and let
\bm{\xi}(P)=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))\in\mathfrak{D}
be
a local minimum of
R_{P}(\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}))
.
Suppose that
\bm{\beta}_{1}(P)=\bm{\beta}^{*}
and
\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}(P))=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
.
Let
\Lambda
be defined by (
8.2
) and (
8.3
) with
\Psi

from (
7.7
).
Then

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))}{\partial\bm{\xi}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}&\mathbf{0}\\
\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}\\
\end{array}\right),

where

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))}{\partial\bm{\beta}}=-\alpha_{1}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right],

(11.33)

with
\alpha_{1}
defined in (
8.5
),
and

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))}{\partial\bm{\gamma}}=\omega_{1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}-\omega_{2}\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathbf{L},

(11.34)

where
\mathbf{L}=\partial\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}(P)))/\partial\bm{\gamma}^{T}
is the
k^{2}\times l
matrix given in (
7.6
),

\omega_{1}=\sigma^{2}(P)|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\gamma_{1}
and
\omega_{2}=\omega_{1}/k+|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}
,
with
\gamma_{1}
defined in (
8.5
).

Proof.

For convenience, write
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
,

\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}(P))
, and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
Write
\partial\Lambda/\partial\bm{\xi}
as the block matrix

\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\xi}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\dfrac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\beta}}&\dfrac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\gamma}}\\
\\
\dfrac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\beta}}&\dfrac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\gamma}}\\
\end{array}\right),

(11.35)

where
\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}
and
\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}
are defined in (
8.3
)
with
\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}
and
\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}
from (
7.7
).
Because
\rho_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfy (R2), (R5), and (V5), and
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
,
according to Lemma
9
we may
we may interchange integration and differentiation
in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}/\partial\bm{\gamma}
and
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}/\partial\bm{\beta}
.
It can be seen that these are expectations of an odd function of
\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
,
which means that they are equal to zero, as
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
is a point of symmetry of
P
.
Therefore

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\xi}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}&\mathbf{0}\\
\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}\\
\end{array}\right).

(11.36)

It remains to determine
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}=\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\bm{\beta}

and
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}=\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\bm{\gamma}
.
According to Lemma
9
, we have that
\Lambda
is continuous differentiable at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
and
that we may interchange
integration and differentiation in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}/\partial\bm{\beta}
, where
\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}
is defined in (
8.3
)
with
\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}
from (
7.7
).
We obtain

\begin{split}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}&=\int\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\beta}}\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})\\
&=-\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma_{P}d_{P}}+u_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\\
&=-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{X}}{\sigma_{P}d_{P}}+u_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right],\end{split}

(11.37)

where
d_{P}^{2}=\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}
and
\mathbf{e}_{P}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
.
The inner expectation on the right hand side is the conditional expectation of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
,
which can be written as

\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1/2}\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1/2}}{\sigma_{P}d_{P}}+u_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{I}_{k}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1/2}\mathbf{X}.

Because
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
and
\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
, the previous expression is equal to

|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{e}^{*}(\mathbf{e}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}}{\sigma_{P}d^{*}}+u_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{I}_{k}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{X},

where
(d^{*})^{2}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})
and
\mathbf{e}^{*}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
.
Note that
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
has the same distribution as
\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
,
where
\mathbf{z}
has a spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
,
so that the expression in the previous display is equal to

|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\frac{c_{\sigma}}{\|\mathbf{z}\|}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}+u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\mathbf{I}_{k}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{X},

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
.
Let
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
.
Then with Lemma
5
we find

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\frac{c_{\sigma}}{\|\mathbf{z}\|}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}+u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\mathbf{I}_{k}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\right]\mathbf{I}_{k}=\alpha_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\alpha_{1}&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{1}{k}u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|+u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{k}\right)\frac{\rho_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)}{c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|}+\frac{1}{k}\rho_{1}^{\prime\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\right].\end{split}

We conclude that

\dfrac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\beta}}=-\alpha_{1}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right].

Next, we determine
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\bm{\gamma}
.
From (
7.7
) we have

\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}=-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{V}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}\right),

for all
j=1,2,\ldots,l
,
where
\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}
is defined in (
7.8
).
Because
|\mathbf{V}_{P}|=1
, we have

\begin{split}\int\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\text{d}P(\mathbf{s})&=\mathbb{E}\left[ku_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}-v_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}_{P}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[ku_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}-v_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}_{P}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right].\end{split}

Similar to the reasoning before, the inner expectation can be written as

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[ku_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\right]\\
&=k\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=k\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\mathbf{0},\end{split}

since
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}]=(1/k)\mathbf{I}_{k}
, according to Lemma
5
.
Hence we conclude that

\int\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\text{d}P(\mathbf{s})=\mathbf{0}.

Since, we may interchange integration and differentiation in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}/\partial\bm{\gamma}
,
according to Lemma
9
,
this means that
for each
j,s=1,\ldots,l
,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\gamma_{s}}&=-\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\gamma_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})\right),\end{split}

(11.38)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}
is defined in (
7.8
).
We have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}}{\partial\gamma_{s}}&=\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{s}}ku_{1}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}-\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{s}}v_{1}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)\sigma^{2}\mathbf{V}+\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{s}}(\log|\mathbf{V}|)\mathbf{V}.\end{split}

(11.39)

Because
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (
7.5
), if follows that
\partial\mathbf{V}/\partial\gamma_{s}=\mathbf{L}_{s}
.
Similar to (
11.37
), for the first term in (
11.39
) we have at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
:

\begin{split}&\int\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{s}}ku_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\,\text{d}P(\mathbf{s})\\
&\quad=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[-ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{1}{2\sigma_{P}d_{P}}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}_{P}}{\partial\gamma_{s}}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right],\end{split}

where
d_{P}^{2}=\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}
and
\mathbf{e}_{P}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
.
Because
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
and
\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
the inner expectation on the right hand side can be written as

\begin{split}&-k\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}}{2\sigma_{P}d^{*}}(\mathbf{e}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{e}^{*}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{*}(\mathbf{e}^{*})^{T}\right]\\
&=-\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}}{2}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right],\end{split}

where
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
.
According to Lemma
5
,
the expectation of the right hand side is equal to

-\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}}{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right].

(11.40)

For the second term in (
11.39
) we get at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
:

\begin{split}&-\int\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{s}}v_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}_{P}\,\text{d}P(\mathbf{s})\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[v_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{1}{2\sigma_{P}d_{P}}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}\cdot\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}_{P}-v_{1}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right],\end{split}

where
d_{P}^{2}=\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}
and
\mathbf{e}_{P}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
.
As before, the inner expectation can be written as

\begin{split}&\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[v_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}{2\sigma_{P}d^{*}}(\mathbf{e}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{e}^{*}\cdot\mathbf{\Sigma}-v_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right]\\
&=\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|}{2}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{\Sigma}-\sigma_{P}^{2}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\mathbf{L}_{s}\right].\end{split}

With Lemma
5
,
for the expectation of the second term in (
11.39
) we get

\begin{split}\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|}{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
-\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\right]\mathbf{L}_{s}.\end{split}

(11.41)

For the third term in (
11.39
) we get at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
:

\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{s}}(\log|\mathbf{V}_{P}|)\mathbf{V}_{P}&=\left(\frac{\partial\log|\mathbf{V}_{P}|}{\partial\gamma_{s}}\right)\mathbf{V}_{P}+\left(\log|\mathbf{V}_{P}|\right)\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}}{\partial\gamma_{s}}\\
&=\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\mathbf{V}_{P}=\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma},\end{split}

(11.42)

using that
|\mathbf{V}_{P}|=1
.
It follows that

\begin{split}&\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\gamma_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})\\
&=-\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}}{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\\
&\qquad+\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|}{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\right]\mathbf{L}_{s}+\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}.\end{split}

(11.43)

In view of (
11.38
) and (
11.43
), for the first term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}/\partial\gamma_{s}

we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\right)\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\right]\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2})\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T})^{T}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})^{T}\frac{1}{k(k+2)}\Big(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}+\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\Big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right),\end{split}

using Lemma
5
.
Application of properties (
11.26
) and (
11.17
), yields

\begin{split}&\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\right)\\
&=\frac{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}}{k(k+2)}\Big(2\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})+\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\Big).\end{split}

It follows that the first term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}/\partial\gamma_{s}
is equal to

\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\right]}{2(k+2)}\Big(2\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})+\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\Big).

(11.44)

Similarly, for the second term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}/\partial\gamma_{s}

we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)^{T}\text{tr}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}).\end{split}

It follows that the second term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}/\partial\gamma_{s}
is equal to

-\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{2k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\right).

(11.45)

Finally, the third term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}/\partial\gamma_{s}
is equal to

\begin{split}&\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{s})\\
&=\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}),\end{split}

(11.46)

and the fourth term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}/\partial\gamma_{s}
is equal to

-|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}(\mathrm{vec}\mathbf{\Sigma})=-|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\right).

(11.47)

We conclude that
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})/\partial\gamma_{s}
consists of
a term

\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})

from (
11.44
) and (
11.46
)
with coefficient

\begin{split}\omega_{1}&=\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\right]}{k+2}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]\right)\\
&=\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}+(k+1)\rho^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{k+2},\end{split}

and a term
-\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})

from (
11.44
), (
11.45
), and (
11.47
)
with coefficient

\begin{split}\omega_{2}&=\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\right]}{2(k+2)}-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{2k}\right)+|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\\
&=\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}+(k+1)\rho^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{k(k+2)}+|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\\
&=\frac{\omega_{1}}{k}+|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}.\end{split}

From the definition of
\mathbf{L}
in (
7.6
) it follows that the
l\times l
matrix with entries

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\gamma_{s}}&=\omega_{1}\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})-\omega_{2}\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}),\end{split}

is the matrix

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\gamma}}=\omega_{1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}-\omega_{2}\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathbf{L}.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
10
hold.
Let
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
be defined in (
11.33
) with
\alpha_{1}
from (
8.5
).
When
\alpha_{1}\neq 0
and
\mathbf{X}
has full rank with probability one,
then
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
is non-singular with inverse

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}^{-1}=-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1}.

Let
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
be defined in (
11.34
)
with
\omega_{1}=\sigma^{2}(P)|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\gamma_{1}
and
\omega_{2}=\omega_{1}/k+|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}
,
where
\gamma_{1}
is defined in (
8.5
) and
\mathbf{L}
is defined in (
7.6
).
When
\gamma_{1}\neq 0
and
\mathbf{L}
has full rank,
then
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
is non-singular with inverse

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1},

where
\mathbf{E}=(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathbf{L}
,

a=1/\omega_{1}
, and
b=\omega_{2}/(\omega_{1}(\omega_{1}-k\omega_{2}))
.

Proof.

Because
\mathbf{X}
has full rank with probability one, it follows that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}]
is non-singular.
Since
\alpha_{1}\neq 0
, this proves part one.
Then consider
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
as given in (
11.34
).
With
\mathbf{E}=(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathbf{L}
,
we can write

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}=\omega_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-\omega_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E},

which is of the form
\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{u}\mathbf{v}^{T}
.
Because
\mathbf{L}
has full rank and
\omega_{1}=\sigma^{2}(P)|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\gamma_{1}\neq 0
, it follows that

\mathbf{A}=\omega_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}=\omega_{1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}
is non-singular.
Furthermore, since
\mathbf{V}
has a linear structure, we have that
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})=\mathbf{L}\bm{\theta}^{*}
,
which implies
\mathbf{E}\bm{\theta}^{*}=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})
and
(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})=\bm{\theta}^{*}
.
This means that
1+\mathbf{v}^{T}\mathbf{A}^{-1}\mathbf{u}=(\omega_{1}-k\omega_{2})/\omega_{1}=k|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}/\omega_{1}\neq 0
.
It then follows from the Sherman-Morisson formula that
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
is non-singular
and has inverse

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1},

where

a=1/\omega_{1}
and
b=\omega_{2}/(\omega_{1}(\omega_{1}-k\omega_{2}))
.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Suppose
P
satisfies (E) for some
(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\bm{\Theta}

and
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
.
Suppose that
\rho_{1}
satisfy (R2) and (R5), and suppose that
\mathbf{V}
satisfy and (V5).
Let
\sigma(P)
be the solution of (
3.8
) and let
\bm{\xi}(P)=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))\in\mathfrak{D}
be
a local minimum of
R_{P}(\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}))
that
satisfies
\bm{\beta}_{1}(P)=\bm{\beta}^{*}
and
\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}(P))=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
.
Then

\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}=\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))}{\partial\sigma}=\mathbf{0}.

Proof.

For convenience, write
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
,

\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}(P))
, and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
Consider
\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}
as defined in (
8.3
) with
\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}

from (
7.7
).
Because
\rho_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfy (R2), (R5), and (V5), and
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
,
according to Lemma
9
,
we may interchange differentiation and integration in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}/\partial\sigma
.
We find

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}&=\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}\,\text{d}P(\mathbf{s})\\
&=-\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}^{2}}\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P})\right].\end{split}

where
d_{P}^{2}=(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P})^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P})
.
Because
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
is a point of symmetry,
it follows that

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta},\sigma}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}=\mathbf{0}.

(11.48)

According to Lemma
9
,
we may also interchange differentiation and integration
in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}/\partial\sigma
,
where
\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}
is defined by (
8.3
) with
\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}

from (
7.7
).
For all
j=1,\ldots,l
we obtain

\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}=-\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})\right),

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}
is defined in (
7.8
).
Since
v_{1}(s)=u_{1}(s)s^{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})&=-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}^{2}}\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}-u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{P}^{3}}{\sigma_{P}^{2}}\mathbf{V}_{P}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\right],\end{split}

where
d^{2}_{P}=\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{P}
and
\mathbf{e}_{P}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
.
The inner expectation on the right hand side is the conditional expectation of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
.
Because
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
and
\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
it follows that

d^{2}_{P}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})
.
Furthermore,
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
has the same distribution as
\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
,
where
\mathbf{z}
has a spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
.
This means that the inner expectation is equal to

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}^{2}}\mathbf{e}_{P}\mathbf{e}_{P}^{T}-u_{1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{d_{P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{d_{P}^{3}}{\sigma_{P}^{2}}\mathbf{V}_{P}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\frac{c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|}{\sigma_{P}}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\frac{\sigma_{P}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\mathbf{\Sigma}\right]\\
&=\frac{\sigma_{P}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[ku_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\mathbf{\Sigma}\right]\end{split}

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
.
Because
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]=(1/k)\mathbf{I}_{k}
, according to Lemma
5
,
the right hand side is equal to

\begin{split}\frac{k\sigma_{P}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}&\\
-\frac{\sigma_{P}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}^{\prime}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{3}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}&=\mathbf{0}.\end{split}

We conclude that

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma},\sigma}=\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\gamma}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\sigma}=\textbf{0}.

(11.49)

Together with (
11.48
) this proves the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6

Proof.

Write
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
,

\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}(P))
, and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
Because
(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\mathbf{V}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k})
,
we have

d_{0,P}^{2}=(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}_{1,P})^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}_{1,P})=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2},

where
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*})
.
This means that

\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})=u_{1}\left(\frac{d_{0,P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{X}_{0}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*})=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\mathbf{X}_{0}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}_{0},

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
.
From Lemmas
9
and
10
,
we have that
\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}
is continuously differentiable at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
,
with a derivative given by

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}=-\alpha_{1}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]
,
which is non-singular according to Lemma
11
.
Because
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
is a point of symmetry, from Theorem
5

we obtain

\begin{split}\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\beta}_{1},P)&=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}^{-1}\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\\
&=\frac{u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)}{\alpha_{1}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}_{0}.\end{split}

This proves part one.

For part two, we apply Lemma
2
(i).
Consider
\Lambda
as defined in (
8.2
) with
\Psi
defined in (
7.7
).
From Lemmas
9
and
10
,
we have that
\Lambda
is continuously differentiable at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
,
with a derivative given by

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}&\mathbf{0}\\
\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}\\
\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
are given in (
11.33
) and (
11.34
).
According to Lemma
11
,

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
are non-singular, which implies that
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}
is non-singular.
Furthermore, from Lemma
12
we have

\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}=\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\sigma=\mathbf{0}
.
From Lemma
2
(i),
this means that

\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P}),

where
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}
is given in Lemma
11
.
The remaining derivation of the expression for
\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)
runs along the same line of reasoning as in
(the second part of) the proof of Corollary 8.4 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
.
Using that
\log|\mathbf{V}_{P}|=0
,
we find that

\begin{split}&\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)\\
&=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\\
&=\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}\left\{ku_{1}\left(\frac{d_{0,P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{e}_{0,P}\mathbf{e}_{0,P}^{T}-v_{1}\left(\frac{d_{0,P}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}_{P}\right\}\\
&=k|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left\{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*}(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*})^{T}\right\}\\
&\qquad-\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}),\end{split}

(11.50)

where
d_{0,P}^{2}=\mathbf{e}_{0,P}^{T}\mathbf{V}_{P}^{-1}\mathbf{e}_{0,P}
with
\mathbf{e}_{0,P}=\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
,
and where
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*}
with
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*}=\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*}
, and
where
c_{\sigma}=\sigma(P)/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}
.
Consider the first term on the right hand side of (
11.50
).
We have that

\begin{split}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}\left\{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*}(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*})^{T}\right\}=\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right).\end{split}

From Lemma
11
we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\\
&=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T},\end{split}

(11.51)

where
\mathbf{E}=(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathbf{L}
,

a=1/\omega_{1}
, and
b=\omega_{2}/(\omega_{1}(\omega_{1}-k\omega_{2}))
.
This implies that

\begin{split}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*}(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{*})^{T}\right)\\
&\quad=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)\\
&\qquad+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right).\end{split}

(11.52)

The first term on the right hand side of (
11.52
) is equal to

\begin{split}a\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L})\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right).\end{split}

Since
\mathbf{V}
has a linear structure, we have
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}_{P})=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\mathbf{L}\bm{\gamma}_{P}
.
This means that

\begin{split}\mathbf{E}\,\bm{\gamma}_{P}&=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{L}\,\bm{\gamma}_{P}\\
&=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k},\end{split}

(11.53)

and

(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}.

(11.54)

It follows that the second term on the right hand side of (
11.52
) is equal to

\begin{split}b\left(|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\right)\left(|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\right)^{T}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)&=b|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)\\
&=b|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)=b|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}.\\
\end{split}

It follows that the first term on the right hand side of (
11.50
) is equal to

\begin{split}&ka|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L}\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad+kb|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{3/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}\bm{\gamma}_{P}.\end{split}

Next, consider the second term on the right hand side of (
11.50
).
From (
11.51
),
together with (
11.53
) and (
11.54
),
we have

\begin{split}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\\
&\quad=\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\\
&\quad=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\\
&\quad=a|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}+b(|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\\
&\quad=a|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}+b(|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P})|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}^{T}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\\
&\quad=a|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}+b(|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\\
&\quad=(a+kb)|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}\bm{\gamma}_{P}.\end{split}

It follows that the second term on the right hand side of (
11.50
) is equal to

-\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)(a+kb)\bm{\gamma}_{P}.

Putting things together, we find that
\mathrm{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)
is equal to

\begin{split}&ka|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L}\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)\\
&\qquad+kb|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{3/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}\bm{\gamma}_{P}-\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)(a+kb)\bm{\gamma}_{P}.\end{split}

The term with
\bm{\gamma}_{P}
has coefficient

\begin{split}&kb|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{3/k}u_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}-\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)(a+kb)\\
&\quad=kb\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)-\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)(a+kb)\\
&\quad=-a\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right),\end{split}

using that
v_{1}(s)=u_{1}(s)s^{2}
.
Note that

a=1/\omega_{1}=1/(\sigma_{P}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\gamma_{1})
.
It follows that

\begin{split}\mathrm{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)&=\frac{ku_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)}{\sigma_{P}^{2}\gamma_{1}}\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L}\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)\\
&\qquad-\frac{v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)}{\gamma_{1}}\bm{\gamma}_{P}.\end{split}

Finally, due to linearity of
\mathbf{V}
,
we have

\mathbf{L}\bm{\gamma}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{P})=\frac{\mathbf{\Sigma}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}=\frac{\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}=\frac{\mathbf{L}\bm{\theta}^{*}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}.

(11.55)

Since
\mathbf{L}
has full rank, we can multiply from the left by
(\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathbf{L})^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}
,
which implies that
\bm{\gamma}_{P}=\bm{\theta}^{*}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
.
This proves the theorem.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4

Proof.

Write
(\bm{\gamma}_{P},\sigma_{P})=(\bm{\gamma}(P),\sigma(P))

and
(\bm{\gamma}_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}},\sigma_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}})=(\bm{\gamma}(P_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}),\sigma(P_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}))
.
Because
\bm{\theta}_{1}(P)
is a solution of (
3.11
) and

\mathbf{V}
has a linear structure, it follows that

\mathbf{L}\bm{\theta}_{1}(P)=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{1}(P))=\sigma^{2}_{P}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{P})=\sigma^{2}_{P}\mathbf{L}\bm{\gamma}_{P}.

(11.56)

Since
\mathbf{L}
has full rank, we can multiply from the left by
(\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathbf{L})^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}
,
which implies that
\bm{\theta}_{1}(P)=\sigma^{2}_{P}\bm{\gamma}_{P}
and
a similar property holds for
\bm{\theta}_{1}(P_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}})
.
We find that

\begin{split}\bm{\theta}_{1}(P_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}})-\bm{\theta}_{1}(P)&=\sigma^{2}_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}\left(\bm{\gamma}_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}-\bm{\gamma}_{P}\right)+\bm{\gamma}_{P}\left(\sigma^{2}_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}-\sigma^{2}_{P}\right)\\
&=\sigma^{2}_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}\left(\bm{\gamma}_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}-\bm{\gamma}_{P}\right)+\bm{\gamma}_{P}\left(\sigma_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}+\sigma_{P}\right)\left(\sigma_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}-\sigma_{P}\right).\end{split}

According to Lemma
7
and Theorem
6
,

\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\sigma,P)
and
\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)
exist.
Together with
\sigma_{h,\mathbf{s}_{0}}\to\sigma_{P}
, we obtain

\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\theta}_{1},P)=\sigma^{2}_{P}\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\gamma},P)+2\sigma_{P}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\sigma,P).

Because
\mathbf{V}
has a linear structure, from (
11.55
),
we have
\bm{\gamma}_{P}=\bm{\theta}^{*}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
after multiplication from the left by
(\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathbf{L})^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}
.
The corollary now follows from the expressions obtained in Lemma
7
and Theorem
6
.
∎

11.6
Influence functions of covariance MM-functionals

We provide some details about the influence functions of the covariance MM-functionals
in Examples
1
,
2
, and
3

for the situation where the distribution
P
satisfies (E).

Example 1

(Linear Mixed Effects model)
.

For the influence function of covariance MM-functionals in linear mixed effects models,
nothing seems to be available yet.
For model (
2.3
), the expression for the influence function of the
variance component MM-functional now follows from Corollary
4
and
the one for the covariance MM-functional and the corresponding shape component follow from (
8.6
)
and (
8.8
), respectively.
From (
7.5
) and (
7.6
), it follows that

\mathbf{L}=\Big[\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)&\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{T}\right)&\cdots&\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{r}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{T}\right)\\
\end{array}\Big].

(11.57)

Furthermore, it can be seen that
\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L}
is equal to the matrix
\mathbf{Q}

with entries

Q_{ij}=\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{Z}_{i}\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{j}\mathbf{Z}_{j}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right),\quad i,j=0,1,\ldots,r,

(11.58)

where
\mathbf{Z}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{k}
, and that

\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{z}_{0}\mathbf{z}_{0}^{T}\right)
is equal to the vector
\mathbf{U}

with coordinates

U_{i}=(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{Z}_{i}\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*}),\quad i=0,1,\ldots,r,

This implies that the influence function of the variance component MM-functional is given by

\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{1},P)=\alpha_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{U}-\beta_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)\bm{\theta}^{*},

with
\alpha_{C}
and
\beta_{C}
defined in (
8.7
),
and
where
\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta}^{*})
and
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma(P)
.
From (
8.6
) we find that the influence functional of the covariance MM-functional is given by

\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{1})),P)=\alpha_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{U}-\beta_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}).

From (
8.8
), we obtain the influence function of the shape MM-functional

\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{1}),P)=\frac{\alpha_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)}{\sigma^{2}(P)}\left\{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{U}-\frac{\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}}{k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\right\},

and in view of Remark
8.1
, from Theorem
6
we obtain
the influence function of the direction MM-functional

\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\gamma},P)=\frac{\alpha_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)}{\sigma^{2}(P)}\left\{\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{U}-\frac{\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}}{k}\bm{\theta}^{*}\right\}.

When
c_{\sigma}=1
, then the influence function of the covariance shape MM-functional
coincides with that of the shape component of the covariance S-functional defined with
\rho_{1}
,
and similarly for the direction component of the variance component S-functional
defined with
\rho_{1}
, see Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
.

Example 2

(Multivariate Linear Regression)
.

For the multivariate linear regression model (
2.4
),
Kudraszow and Maronna
[
15
]
do not consider the
influence function of the covariance MM-functional.
In this model, the matrix
\mathbf{L}
is equal to the duplication matrix
\mathcal{D}_{k}
,
which satisfies

\mathcal{D}_{k}\left(\mathcal{D}_{k}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{k}\right)^{-1}\mathcal{D}_{k}^{T}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}\right)(\mathbf{\Sigma}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}),

(11.59)

(e.g., see Magnus and Neudecker
[
24
, Ch. 3, Sec. 8]
).
Together with (
8.6
) we find that the expression for
the covariance MM-functional is given by

\begin{split}\text{\rm IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{1}),P)&=\alpha_{C}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0})^{T}-\beta_{C}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|)\mathbf{\Sigma},\end{split}

(11.60)

with
\alpha_{C}
and
\beta_{C}
defined in (
8.7
),

\mathbf{z}_{0}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0})
, and
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma(P)
.
From (
8.8
), it follows that
the influence function of the shape MM-functional is given by

\text{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0};\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{1}),P)=\frac{\alpha_{C}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|\right)}{\sigma^{2}(P)}\left\{(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0})^{T}-\frac{\|\mathbf{z}_{0}\|^{2}}{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}\right\}.

(11.61)

When
c_{\sigma}=1
,
this also coincides with the influence function of
shape component of the covariance S-functional defined with
\rho_{1}
in the
multivariate linear regression model.
This is confirmed by application of formula (8.3) in Kent and Tyler
[
14
]

to the expression found in Theorem 2 of Van Aelst and Willems
[
35
]
.

Example 3

(Multivariate Location and Scatter)
.

For the multivariate location-scatter model, we also have
\mathbf{L}=\mathcal{D}_{k}
.
Since this model is a special case of the multivariate linear regression model (
2.4
)
by taking
\mathbf{x}_{i}=1
and
\mathbf{B}^{T}=\bm{\mu}
,
the expression for the influence function of the covariance MM-functional can
be obtained from (
11.60
)
and the influence function of the covariance shape MM-functional from (
11.61
),
by replacing
\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0}
by
\bm{\mu}
.
When
c_{\sigma}=1
, this also coincides with the influence function of
shape component of the covariance S-functional defined with
\rho_{1}
in the multivariate location-scatter model.
This was already observed by Salibián-Barrera
et al

[
31
]
.
Finally, again there is a connection with the CM-functionals considered in Kent and Tyler
[
14
]
,
whose influence function depends on a parameter
\lambda_{0}
.
By using (
11.59
), one finds that the expression in (
11.60
),
with
\mathbf{B}^{T}\mathbf{x}_{0}=\bm{\mu}
,
is similar to the expression for the influence function of the covariance CM-functional,
for the case that
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{L}
(see Kent and Tyler
[
14
]
for details),
and they both coincide when
c_{\sigma}=1/\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}
and
\rho_{0}=\rho_{1}
.

11.7
Proofs for Section
9

Lemma 13
.

Suppose that
\rho_{1}
satisfies (R1)-(R4), such that
u_{1}(s)
is of bounded variation,
and suppose that
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4).
Let
\sigma_{n}
and
\sigma(P)
be solutions of (
3.2
) and (
3.8
), respectively,
and let
\bm{\xi}_{n}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,n},\bm{\gamma}_{n})
and
\bm{\xi}(P)=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
be local minima of
R_{n}(\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}))
and

R_{P}(\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}))
, respectively.
Suppose that
(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})\to(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))
, in probability.
Let
\Lambda
be defined in (
8.2
) with
\Psi
defined in (
7.3
)
Suppose that
\Lambda
is continuously differentiable with
a non-singular derivative
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}=\partial\Lambda/\partial\bm{\xi}

and derivative
\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}=\partial\Lambda/\partial\sigma

at
(\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))
,
and suppose that
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{s}\|^{2}<\infty
.
Then

\begin{split}\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}(P)&=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\bigg\{\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P))+\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}(P),\sigma(P))\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\bigg\}\\
&\quad+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}(P)\|)+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),\end{split}

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem 9.1 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
.
Due to (
3.12
), we have that
\bm{\xi}_{n}=\bm{\xi}(\mathbb{P}_{n})=(\bm{\beta}_{1,n},\bm{\gamma}_{n})
and
\sigma_{n}=\sigma(\mathbb{P}_{n})
.
This means that
(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})
satisfies score equation (
7.2
) for the MM-functionals at
\mathbb{P}_{n}
, that is

\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\mathbf{s})=\mathbf{0},

with
\Psi
defined in (
7.3
).
Writing
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
, we decompose as follows

\begin{split}\mathbf{0}=\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})&+\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
&+\int\left(\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s}).\end{split}

(11.62)

We start by showing that the third term on the right hand side of (
11.62
)
is of order
o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
Since
\Psi=(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}},\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}})
, with
\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}=(\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},1},\ldots,\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},l})
,
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\int\left(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),

(11.63)

\displaystyle\int\left(\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),

(11.64)

for
j=1,\ldots,l
.
From (
7.3
) we have that

\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=u_{1}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})=u_{1}\left(d(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta},\sigma^{2}\mathbf{V})\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}).

Since
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V1) we have that
\sigma_{n}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})\to\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{P})
, in probability.
Then (
11.63
) follows from equation (96) in the proof of Lemma 11.8 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
17
]
.
From (
7.3
), we also have

\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=u_{1}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{1,j}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})-\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{V}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\right)\log|\mathbf{V}|,

for
j=1,\ldots,l
,
where
\mathbf{H}_{1,j}=\mathbf{H}_{1,j}(\bm{\gamma})
is defined in (
7.4
)
Because
|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})|=1
, it follows that

\begin{split}\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})=u_{1}\left(d_{n}\right)(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,n})^{T}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{1,j}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,n}),\end{split}

where
d_{n}^{2}=(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,n})^{T}(\sigma_{n}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}))^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,n})

and similarly for
\Psi_{\bm{\gamma},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
.
Because
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4) we have that
\sigma_{n}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})\to\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{P})
and

\mathbf{H}_{1,j}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})\to\mathbf{H}_{1,j}(\bm{\gamma}_{P})
, in probability.
This implies that (
11.64
) follows from equation (97) in the proof of Lemma 11.8 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
17
]
.

Then, from (
11.62
) we can write

\mathbf{0}=\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})+\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

Because
\Lambda
is continuously differentiable at

(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
, it follows that

\begin{split}\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})&=\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{n})+\frac{\partial\Lambda}{\partial\bm{\xi}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{n})(\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}\|)\\
&=\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{n})+\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda}{\partial\bm{\xi}}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(1)\right)(\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}\|).\end{split}

Furthermore, since
\bm{\xi}_{P}
is a solution of (
7.2
), we find

\begin{split}\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{n})&=\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})+\frac{\partial\Lambda}{\partial\sigma}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|)\\
&=\frac{\partial\Lambda}{\partial\sigma}(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|).\end{split}

Putting things together, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbf{0}=\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})&+\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}(\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P})+\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
&+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}\|)+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

Because
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}
is non-singular, it follows that

\begin{split}\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\bigg\{\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})&+\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\bigg\}\\
&+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}\|)+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Suppose that
\rho_{0}
satisfies (R1)-(R2) and
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4).
Let
\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}=(\bm{\beta}_{0,n},\bm{\theta}_{0,n})

and
\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P)=(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))
be the pairs of initial estimators and
corresponding functionals, and let
\sigma_{n}
and
\sigma(P)
be solutions of (
3.2
) and (
3.8
), respectively.
Suppose that
(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})\to(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P)
), in probability.
Let
\Lambda_{0}
be defined in (
11.22
) with
\Psi_{0}
defined in (
11.21
),
and suppose that
\Lambda_{0}

is continuously differentiable with derivatives

D_{0,\sigma}=\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\sigma\neq 0

and

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}=\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\bm{\zeta}

at
(\bm{\zeta}(P),\sigma(P))
.
Then

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)&=-D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\bigg\{\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}^{T}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P))+\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\bigg\}\\
&\quad+o(\|\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P)\|)+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

Proof.

Denote
\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}=(\bm{\beta}_{0,n},\bm{\theta}_{0,n})
and
let
\sigma_{n}
and
\sigma(P)
be solutions of (
3.2
) and (
3.8
), respectively.
Then, according to (
3.2
),
(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})
satisfies

\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}_{n}(\mathbf{s})=0.

Writing
\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}=(\bm{\beta}_{0,P},\bm{\theta}_{0,P})
and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
, we decompose as follows

\begin{split}\mathbf{0}=\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})+\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
+\int\left(\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s}).\end{split}

(11.65)

Consider the third term on the right hand side, where we can write
