\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta},\sigma)=\rho_{0}\left(d(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta},\sigma^{2}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}))\right)-b_{0},

where
d(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta},\sigma^{2}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}))
is defined in (
7.1
)
and
\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta})/|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta})|^{1/k}
.
Because
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4) we have that
\sigma_{n}^{2}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,n})\to\sigma_{P}^{2}\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0,P})
, in probability.
Since
\rho_{0}
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfy the conditions needed to establish (98) in the proof of
Lemma 11.8 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
17
]
, it follows that

\int\left(\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

Then from (
11.65
) we can write

0=\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})+\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

Because the partial derivative
\partial\Lambda_{0}/\partial\sigma
is continuous at

(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})
, it follows that

\begin{split}\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{n})&=\Lambda_{0}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{P})+\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}}{\partial\sigma}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{P})(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|)\\
&=\Lambda(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{P})+D_{0,\sigma}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|).\end{split}

Furthermore, since
\sigma_{P}
is a solution of (
3.8
), we find

\begin{split}\Lambda(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n},\sigma_{P})&=\Lambda(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})+\frac{\partial\Lambda_{0}}{\partial\bm{\zeta}}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}\|)\\
&=\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}^{T}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}\|).\end{split}

Putting things together gives

\begin{split}0=\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}^{T}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P})&+D_{0,\sigma}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
&+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}\|)+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|).\end{split}

Because
D_{0,\sigma}\neq 0
, it follows that

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}=-D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\bigg\{\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}^{T}(\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P})+\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\bigg\}\\
+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}\|)+o_{P}(|\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}|).\end{split}

This proves the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7

Proof.

Write
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
Similar to (
11.62
) we decompose as follows

\begin{split}\mathbf{0}=\int\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})&+\int\Psi{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
&+\int\left(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s}).\end{split}

From (
7.3
) we have that

\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=u_{1}\left(\frac{d}{\sigma}\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})=u_{1}\left(d(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta},\sigma^{2}\mathbf{V})\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}).

Since
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V1) we have that
\sigma_{n}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{n})\to\sigma_{P}^{2}\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{P})
, in probability.
Then similar to the proof of equation (96) in Lemma 11.8 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
17
]
,
it follows

\int\left(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})-\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

This means we can write

\mathbf{0}=\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})+\int\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

(11.66)

Since
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}/\partial\bm{\beta}
is continuous at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})

and
(\bm{\gamma}_{n},\sigma_{n})\to(\bm{\gamma}_{P},\sigma_{P})
, in probability, it follows that

\begin{split}\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})&=\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\gamma_{n},\sigma_{n})+\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}}{\partial\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P},\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(1)\right)(\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P}).\end{split}

Because
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
is a point of symmetry and
\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}
is an
odd function of
\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}
,
it follows that
\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{n},\sigma_{n})=\mathbf{0}
,
so that

\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}_{n},\sigma_{n})=\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P})+o(\|\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P}\|).

Together with (
11.66
), we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbf{0}=\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\beta}_{n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P})&+\int\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o(\|\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P}\|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

Since
\rho_{1}
satisfies (R2) and (R4),
according to Lemma
8
there exist a constant
C_{1}>0
only depending on
P
and
\rho_{1}
, such that

\|\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s};\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\|\leq C_{1}\|\mathbf{X}\|
.
Since
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
, the central limit theorem applies to the second term on the right hand side.
From this, we first conclude that
\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
After inserting this in the previous equality and use that
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
is non-singular, this implies

\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}^{-1}\int\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 15
.

Suppose that
P
satisfies (E) for some
(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\bm{\Theta}

and suppose that
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|<\infty
.
Suppose that
\rho_{0}
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfy (R2), (R4) and (V4), respectively.
Let
\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}=(\bm{\beta}_{0,n},\bm{\theta}_{0,n})
be the pair of initial estimators
and let
\bm{\zeta}_{0}=(\bm{\beta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{0})
be the corresponding functional
satisfying
(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})
, and suppose that
\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
Let
\sigma_{n}
be a solution of (
3.2
)
and suppose that
\sigma_{n}\to\sigma(P)
, in probability,
where
\sigma(P)
is a solution of (
3.8
).
Suppose that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]>0
,
where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma(P)
.
Then
\sqrt{n}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P))
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\frac{\sigma^{2}(P)\mathbb{E}\left[(\rho_{0}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)-b_{0})^{2}\right]}{\big(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]\big)^{2}}.

Proof.

We apply Lemma
14
.
Consider
\Lambda_{0}
as defined in (
11.22
) with
\Psi_{0}
from (
11.21
).
From Lemma
4
, we have that

\Lambda_{0}
is continuously differentiable at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))
,
with derivatives
\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}=\mathbf{0}
and

D_{0,\sigma}=-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]/\sigma(P)<0
,
according to Lemma
6
.
Since
\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
,
from Lemma
14
, it then follows that

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)=-D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
+o_{P}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P))+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),\end{split}

(11.67)

Since
\rho_{0}
is bounded, the central limit theorem applies
to the first term on the right hand side of (
11.67
).
We first conclude that
\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
and
after inserting this in (
11.67
),
we find that

\sqrt{n}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P))
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))^{2}\right]/D_{0,\sigma}^{2}
.
Since
(\bm{\beta}_{0}(P),\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=(\bm{\beta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{*})
and

\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{0}(P))=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})/|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}^{*})|^{1/k}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
it follows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\rho_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma(P)}\right)-b_{0}\right)^{2}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right],

where
d_{\Gamma}^{*}
is defined in (
11.29
).
The inner expectation on the right hand side
is the conditional expectation of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
,
which has the same distribution as

\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
, where
\mathbf{z}
has spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
.
This implies that the inner expectation on the right hand side is equal to

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[(\rho_{0}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)-b_{0})^{2}]
,
where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma(P)
.
This proves the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8

Proof.

Write
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
,

\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{p})
, and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
We apply Lemma
13
.
Consider
\Lambda
as defined in (
8.2
) with
\Psi
as defined in (
7.7
).
From Lemmas
9
and
10
,
we have that
\Lambda
is continuously differentiable at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})

with derivative

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}=\frac{\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})}{\partial\bm{\xi}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}&\mathbf{0}\\
\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}\\
\end{array}\right),

where
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
are given in (
11.33
) and (
11.34
).
According to Lemma
11
, we have that

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}
are non-singular, so that
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}
is non-singular.
Furthermore, from Lemma
12
, we have that

\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}=\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\sigma=\mathbf{0}
.
Since
\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
,
Lemma
13
yields that

\begin{split}\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}&=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}(P)\|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

According to Lemma
8
, there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
only depending on
P
and
\sigma(P)
, such that

\left\|\Psi(\mathbf{s};\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right\|\leq C_{1}+C_{2}\|\mathbf{X}\|
.
Since
\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{X}\|^{2}<\infty
, the central limit theorem applies to the second term on the right hand side.
From this we first obtain that
\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
After inserting this in the previous equation, we find that

\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

(11.68)

It follows that
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P})
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}\right]\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}.

(11.69)

Because
\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}
is
an odd function of
\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{1,P}
and
\bm{\beta}_{1,P}=\bm{\beta}^{*}
is a point of symmetry,
it follows that

\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}]=\mathbf{0}
.
Hence, also
\mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}]

is a block matrix with

\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}]

and

\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}]

on the main diagonal.
We conclude that the limiting variance (
11.69
) of
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P})

is a block matrix.
This proves that
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P})
and

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})
are asymptotically independent.

Moreover, it also follows that
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1,P})
is asymptotically normal with
mean zero and variance

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}]\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\beta}}^{-1},

(11.70)

and
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}]\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}.

(11.71)

Because
\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
,
we can write

\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}u_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}),

where
(d^{*})^{2}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})
and

u_{1}(s)=\rho_{1}^{\prime}(s)/s
.
We find that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}\right]\\
&\qquad=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\mathbf{X}\right].\end{split}

The inner expectation on the right hand side
is the conditional expectation of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
,
which has the same distribution as

\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
, where
\mathbf{z}
has spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
.
Therefore,
similar to the proof of Theorem
6
,
the inner conditional expectation can be written as

\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]}{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1},

where
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
.
Together with (
11.33
)
and
u_{1}(s)=\rho_{1}^{\prime}(s)/s
,
this implies that
the asymptotic variance (
11.70
) of
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\beta}_{1,n}-\bm{\beta}_{1}(P))
is given by

\frac{\sigma_{P}^{2}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho_{1}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\right]}{k\alpha_{1}^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1},

where
\alpha_{1}
is defined in (
8.5
).

Next, consider the limiting variance (
11.71
) of
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}(P))
.
According to Lemma
11
, we have

\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1},

(11.72)

where
\mathbf{E}=(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathbf{L}
, and

a=1/\omega_{1}
and
b=\omega_{2}(\omega_{1}(\omega_{1}-k\omega_{2}))
,
where
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
are given in Lemma
10
.
The rest of the proof is similar to the (second part of the) proof of Corollary 9.2 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
.
To make the connection with the proof of Corollary 9.2 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
,
note that
\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{\Sigma}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
, so that

\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})=-|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right),

and

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right)^{T}\right]\\
&=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{4/k}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\right]\mathbf{E},\end{split}

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}
is given in (
7.8
).
Furthermore, with
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
,
we can write

\begin{split}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}&=ku_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}-v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\frac{\sigma_{P}^{2}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=ku_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}-v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\Big\{ku_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}-v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)\mathbf{\Sigma}\Big\}.\end{split}

Similar to the proof of Corollary 9.2 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}\right]&=\sigma_{P}^{4}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\Big\{2\delta_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}+\delta_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}\Big\},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\delta_{1}&=\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{4}\right]}{k+2}\\
\delta_{2}&=\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{4}\right]}{k+2}-2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)v_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\right]\\
&\phantom{=\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{4}\right]}{k+2}}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}(\|c_{\sigma}\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\right].\end{split}

Because
v_{1}(s)=u_{1}(s)s^{2}
, we find that

\frac{k}{k+2}u_{1}(s)^{2}s^{4}-2u_{1}(s)v_{1}(s)s^{2}+v_{1}(s)^{2}=-\frac{2}{k+2}u_{1}(s)^{2}s^{4}.

This means

\begin{split}\delta_{1}&=\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{4}\right]}{k+2}\\
\delta_{2}&=-\frac{2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{4}\right]}{k+2}=-\frac{2}{k}\delta_{1}.\end{split}

(11.73)

Together with (
11.72
),
as in the proof of Corollary 9.2 in Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
,
it follows that

\begin{split}&\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}\right]\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\\
&=2\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+\sigma_{2}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1},\end{split}

where

\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{P}^{4}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}a^{2}\delta_{1}=\frac{\delta_{1}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\gamma_{1}^{2}},

and

\sigma_{2}=\sigma_{P}^{4}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\left\{2b(2a+kb)\delta_{1}+(a+kb)^{2}\delta_{2}\right\}=-2\sigma_{P}^{4}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}a^{2}\delta_{1}/k=-2\sigma_{1}/k.

We obtain

\begin{split}\sigma_{1}&=\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u_{1}(\|c_{\sigma}\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{4}\right]}{(k+2)\gamma_{1}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}};\\
\sigma_{2}&=-\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{k}.\end{split}

(11.74)

Since
\mathbf{V}
has a linear structure, we have
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})=\mathbf{L}\bm{\theta}^{*}

and
\mathbf{E}\bm{\theta}^{*}=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})
and

(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})=\bm{\theta}^{*}.

We find that the limiting variance (
11.71
) is equal to

2\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L})^{-1}+\sigma_{2}\bm{\theta}^{*}(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
5

Proof.

Write
\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}=\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P)
,
\bm{\xi}_{P}=(\bm{\beta}_{1,P},\bm{\gamma}_{P})=(\bm{\beta}_{1}(P),\bm{\gamma}(P))
,

\mathbf{V}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\gamma}_{p})
, and
\sigma_{P}=\sigma(P)
.
Because
\bm{\theta}_{1,n}
and
\bm{\theta}_{1}(P)
are solutions of (
3.6
) and (
3.11
), respectively,
similar to the reasoning in the proof of Corollary
4
,
we have that

\begin{split}\bm{\theta}_{1,n}-\bm{\theta}_{1}(P)&=\sigma_{n}^{2}\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\sigma_{P}^{2}\bm{\gamma}_{P}=\sigma_{n}^{2}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})+\bm{\gamma}_{P}(\sigma_{n}^{2}-\sigma_{P}^{2})\\
&=\sigma_{n}^{2}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})+\bm{\gamma}_{P}(\sigma_{n}+\sigma_{P})(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}).\end{split}

(11.75)

We will apply Lemmas
13
and
14
.
From Lemma
4
,
we have that
\Lambda_{0}
is continuously differentiable at
(\bm{\zeta}_{0,P},\sigma_{P})
with derivatives

\mathbf{D}_{0,\bm{\zeta}}=\mathbf{0}
and

D_{0,\sigma}<0
, according to Lemma
6
.
Since
\bm{\zeta}_{0,n}-\bm{\zeta}_{0,P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
, it follows from
Lemma
14
that

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)=-D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
+o_{P}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

Since
\rho_{0}
is bounded, the central limit theorem applies
to the first term on the right hand side of (
11.76
)
and we find that
\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
Therefore,

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}-\sigma(P)=-D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\int\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\zeta}_{0}(P),\sigma(P))\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

(11.76)

From Lemma
9
,
we have that
\Lambda
is continuous differentiable at
(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})
,
with derivative
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}=\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\bm{\xi}
given in
Lemma
10
,
which is non-singular according to Lemma
11
.
Furthermore, according to Lemma
12
, we have
\mathbf{D}_{\sigma}=\partial\Lambda(\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})/\partial\sigma=\mathbf{0}
.
Since
\rho_{1}
satisfies (R1)-(R4), such that
u_{1}(s)
is of bounded variation,
and
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4), we may apply Lemma
13
and obtain

\begin{split}\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}&=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(\|\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}(P)\|)+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).\end{split}

As in the proof of Theorem
8
,
we first obtain that
\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
and then conclude that

\bm{\xi}_{n}-\bm{\xi}_{P}=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}^{-1}\int\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

From the block structure of
\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\xi}}
established in Lemma
10
,
it then follows that

\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P}=-\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\int\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})+o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

(11.77)

In particular, this implies that
\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P}=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
, so that
from (
11.75
) we obtain

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\theta}_{1,n}-\bm{\theta}_{1}(P))=\sigma^{2}_{P}\sqrt{n}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})+2\sigma_{P}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\sqrt{n}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})+o_{P}(1).

From expansions (
11.76
) and (
11.77
),
we conclude that if we define

\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=\sigma^{2}_{P}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})+2\sigma_{P}\bm{\gamma}_{P}D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P}),

then
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\theta}_{1,n}-\bm{\theta}_{1}(P))
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}\right].

As in the proof of Theorem
8
,

\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})=-|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\right),

with

\begin{split}\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})&=ku_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}-v_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{P}^{2}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}\mathbf{\Sigma}.\end{split}

where
(d^{*})^{2}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*})
,
and from the proof of Lemma
15

\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})=\rho_{0}\left(\frac{d_{\Gamma}^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)-b_{0},

where
d_{\Gamma}^{*}=d^{*}
.
This means that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(ku_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{e}^{*}(\mathbf{e}^{*})^{T}-\frac{\sigma_{P}^{2}}{|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}}v_{1}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(\frac{d^{*}}{\sigma_{P}}\right)-b_{0}\right)\bigg|\mathbf{X}\right]\right],\end{split}

where
\mathbf{e}^{*}=\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
.
The inner expectation is the conditional expectation of
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
,
which has the same distribution as

\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}+\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}^{*}
, where
\mathbf{z}
has spherical density
f_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}
.
Therefore, if we denote
c_{\sigma}=|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/(2k)}/\sigma_{P}
, then the inner expectation can be written as

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\left(ku_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}-v_{1}\left(\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[ku_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}|\mathbf{z}\|\right)(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\frac{k}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\frac{k}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\right]\frac{1}{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v_{1}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)\left(\rho_{0}\left(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|\right)-b_{0}\right)\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}=\mathbf{0}.\end{split}

We find that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)^{T}\right]=\sigma^{4}_{P}\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{T}\right]\mathbf{D}_{\bm{\gamma}}^{-1}\\
+4\sigma^{2}_{P}D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{0}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P},\sigma_{P})^{2}\right]D_{0,\sigma}^{-1}\bm{\gamma}_{P}\bm{\gamma}_{P}^{T},\end{split}

which is a linear combination
of the asymptotic variances of

\sqrt{n}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})
and
\sqrt{n}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})
:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi},\sigma)^{T}\right]\\
&\qquad=\sigma^{4}_{P}\text{AVAR}\left(\sqrt{n}(\bm{\gamma}_{n}-\bm{\gamma}_{P})\right)+4\sigma^{2}_{P}\text{AVAR}\left(\sqrt{n}(\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{P})\right)\bm{\gamma}_{P}\bm{\gamma}_{P}^{T}.\end{split}

Since
\mathbf{V}
has a linear structure, similar to the proof of Corollary
4
,
from (
11.55
) we have
\bm{\gamma}_{P}=\bm{\theta}^{*}/|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{1/k}
.
Then, from Theorem
8
and Lemma
15
,
we find that the right hand side is equal to

\begin{split}&\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\left\{\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}\Big)^{-1}-\frac{1}{k}\bm{\theta}^{*}(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}\right\}+\frac{4}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[(\rho_{0}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)-b_{0})^{2}\right]}{\big(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\rho_{0}^{\prime}(c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|)c_{\sigma}\|\mathbf{z}\|]\big)^{2}}\bm{\theta}^{*}(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof.
∎

11.8
Limiting distributions of covariance estimators

We provide some details about the limiting variances of the covariance MM-estimators
in Examples
1
,
2
, and
3

for the situation where the distribution
P
satisfies (E).

Example 1

(Linear Mixed Effects model)
.

For linear mixed effects models,
nothing seems to be available about the limiting distribution of covariance MM-estimators.
For model (
2.3
), the limiting distribution of the
variance component MM-estimator now follows from Corollary
5
and
the limiting distributions of the covariance MM-estimator
and the corresponding shape component follow from (
9.4
)
and (
9.5
), respectively.
This implies that
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\theta}_{1,n}-\bm{\theta}_{1}(P))
is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}+\left(-\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{kc_{\sigma}^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\right)\bm{\theta}^{*}(\bm{\theta}^{*})^{T},

where
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{3}
are defined in (
9.2
) and (
9.3
),
and where
\mathbf{Q}
is the matrix with entries given in (
11.58
).
From (
9.4
) we find that

\sqrt{n}(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{1,n}))-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}))
is asymptotically normal with
mean zero and variance

\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}+\left(-\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{kc_{\sigma}^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})^{T},

where
\mathbf{L}
is given by (
11.57
).
For the shape component
\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta})=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta})/|\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta})|^{1/k}
,
from (
9.5
) we obtain that

\sqrt{n}(\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{1,n}))-\mathrm{vec}(\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{\theta}_{1}(P))))

is asymptotically normal with mean zero and variance

\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{c_{\sigma}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}}\left\{\mathbf{L}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}-\frac{1}{k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})^{T}\right\}.

When
c_{\sigma}=1
, the limiting distribution
of the covariance shape MM-estimator in the linear mixed effects model (
2.3
)
coincides with that of the shape component corresponding to the covariance S-estimator defined with
\rho_{1}
,
see Lopuhaä
et al

[
22
]
,
and similarly for the direction component of the variance component MM-estimator.

Example 2

(Multivariate Linear Regression)
.

For the multivariate linear regression model (
2.4
),
Kudraszow and Maronna
[
15
]
do not consider the
limiting distribution of the covariance MM-estimator.
In this model, the matrix
\mathbf{L}
is equal to the duplication matrix
\mathcal{D}_{k}
,
which satisfies (
11.59
).
The limiting distribution of the covariance MM-estimator
and the corresponding shape component follow from (
9.4
)
and (
9.5
), respectively.
By using (
11.59
), the limiting variance of the covariance MM-estimator becomes

\frac{\sigma_{1}}{c_{\sigma}^{2}}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}\right)(\mathbf{\Sigma}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma})+\left(-\frac{2\sigma_{1}}{kc_{\sigma}^{2}}+\sigma_{3}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})^{T},

(11.78)

whereas the covariance shape estimator has limiting variance

\frac{\sigma_{1}}{c_{\sigma}^{2}|\mathbf{\Sigma}|^{2/k}}\left\{\left(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}\right)(\mathbf{\Sigma}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma})-\frac{2}{k}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})^{T}\right\},

(11.79)

where
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{3}
are defined in (
9.2
) and (
9.3
).
When
c_{\sigma}=1
, the limit behavior of the covariance shape MM-estimator
coincides with that of the shape component of the covariance S-estimator defined with
\rho_{1}

in the multivariate linear regression model.

Example 3

(Multivariate Location and Scatter)
.

For the multivariate location-scatter model, we also have
\mathbf{L}=\mathcal{D}_{k}
.
Since this model is a special case of the multivariate linear regression model (
2.4
)
by taking
\mathbf{x}_{i}=1
and
\mathbf{B}^{T}=\bm{\mu}
,
the limiting distributions of the covariance MM-estimator and the corresponding shape component
are the same as that of their counterparts in the multivariate linear regression model
and the limiting variances have the same expressions as (
11.78
)
and (
11.79
).
When
c_{\sigma}=1
, the behavior of the covariance shape MM-estimator
coincides with that of the covariance shape S-estimator defined with
\rho_{1}
.
This was already observed by Salibián-Barrera
et al

[
31
]
,
whose formula (9) matches with the expression in (
11.79
) with
c_{\sigma}=1
.
Finally, also here there is a connection with the CM-estimators considered in
Kent and Tyler
[
14
]
,
whose limiting distribution depends on a parameter
\lambda_{0}
.
By using (
11.59
), it can be seen that the limiting distribution
of
\sqrt{n}(\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{1,n}))-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}))
is similar to that of
the covariance CM-estimator for the particular case that
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{L}
(see Kent and Tyler
[
14
]
for details),
and that they both coincide when
c_{\sigma}=1/\sqrt{\lambda_{0}}
and
\rho_{0}=\rho_{1}
.
